According to fMRI data and other methods, the left hemisphere is dominant for speech production in more than 95 percent of right-handers and nearly 80 percent of left-handers.","Some strongly left-handed people have right-hemisphere dominance for speech, but most left-handers have either left-hemisphere control or a mixture of left- and right-hemisphere control.","Division of labor between the two hemispheres is called lateralization. Only after damage to the corpus callosum or to one hemisphere do we see clear evidence of lateralization.","The planum temporale is also larger on the left side in most infants as well.","Even 2-month-old children activate the left hemisphere more than the right when they listen to speech, and not when they listen to music.","of them gradually suppress the right hemisphere during speech and emphasize the left hemisphere.","In short, the left hemisphere is predisposed to dominate for speech, and ordinarily it gradually suppresses the speech capacity of the right hemisphere. Smaller differences are found between left and right hemispheres of chimpanzees, bonobos, and gorillas.","Evidently, the specialization we see in the human brain built upon specializations present in our apelike ancestors of long ago.","The hemispheres connect to the eyes such that each hemisphere sees the opposite half of the visual world.","In rabbits and other species with eyes far to the side of the head, the left eye connects to the right hemisphere, and the right eye connects to the left.","Light from the right half of the visual field (what one sees) strikes the left half of each retina, which connects to the left hemisphere, enabling it to see the right visual field.","A vertical strip down the center of each retina, covering about 5 degrees of visual arc, connects to both hemispheres. In Figure 13.3, note how approximately half of the axons from each eye cross to the opposite side of the brain at the optic chiasm (literally, the “optic cross”).","Each ear sends some of the information to both sides of the brain, because any brain area that contributes to localizing sounds must compare input from both ears.","Epilepsy is a condition characterized by repeated episodes of excessive synchronized neural activity.","In certain cases when removing the focus was impossible, surgeons cut the corpus callosum to prevent epileptic seizures from crossing from one hemisphere to the other.","One benefit was that, as predicted, the person’s epileptic seizures affected only half the body. (The abnormal activity could not cross the corpus callosum.) A surprising bonus was that the seizures became less frequent.","However, they struggle to use the hands together on tasks that they have not previously practiced. People with split-brain syndrome can use the two hands independently in ways that other people cannot.","Most people find this task difficult, but those with split-brain do it with ease.","Most people find this task difficult, but splitbrain people spontaneously draw circles at different speeds.","Information going to one hemisphere could not cross to the other, because of the damage to the corpus callosum. However, when the right hemisphere perceived something, the person would point to it with the left hand, while saying, “I don’t know what it was.","Because the corpus callosum develops slowly, behaviors of young children occasionally resemble those of split-brain adults.","In the first weeks after split-brain surgery, the hemispheres act like separate people sharing one body.","One person with split-brain repeatedly took items from the grocery shelf with one hand and returned them with the other.","She explained, “I’d reach with my right for the thing I wanted, but the left would come in and they’d kind of fight.” She had similar problems when If not, the right hemisphere, knowing the correct answer, makes the face frown. (Both hemispheres control facial muscles on both sides of the face.) The left hemisphere, feeling the frown, says, “Oh, I’m sorry, I meant ‘no.’” When the right hemisphere does something, the left hemisphere doesn’t know why.","From observations like these, Michael Gazzaniga proposed the concept of the interpreter, the tendency of the left hemisphere to invent and defend explanations for actions, even when the true causes are unconscious.","Gradually observations revealed the right hemisphere’s own important functions.","Then turn left and go to the corner that has a flagpole and turn right...”.","The right hemisphere is more responsive than the left is to the emotional tone of communication, which people express with gestures and tone of voice.","People with damage to the right hemisphere usually fail to understand humor and sarcasm. Happier mood tends to correlate with left-hemisphere activity, and depression is associated with increased activity in the right prefrontal cortex.","Increased right-hemisphere activity is also common in people who have recovered from depression, implying that it is a long-term predisposing factor rather than a reaction to depression.","Most people gaze to the right during verbal tasks, suggesting left hemisphere dominance, but most individuals with depression gaze to the left.","People with an intact left hemisphere relied on the left hemisphere’s analysis of what people were saying. Those with left-hemisphere damage relied on the right hemisphere’s more intuitive reactions to emotional expressions.","Evidently, when the right hemisphere is inactive, people do not experience strong emotions and do not even remember feeling them.","The research on left-brain/right-brain differences is exciting, but has sometimes led to unscientific assertions.","Occasionally, you may hear a person say something like, “I don’t do well in science because it is a left-brain subject, and I am a right-brain person.” That kind of statement is based on two reasonable premises and one doubtful one.","The scientific ideas are that (1) the hemispheres are specialized for different functions and (2) certain tasks evoke greater activity in one hemisphere or the other. Generally, when people say, “I am right-brained,” their only evidence is that they perform well on creative tasks or poorly on logical tasks.","Saying, “I am right-brained” sometimes implies that because I do poorly on logical tasks, therefore, I am creative.","During your childhood, you may have heard, watched, or read stories and cartoons about animals.","Nonhuman animals do communicate through visual, auditory, tactile, or chemical displays.","Probably all vertebrates can learn the meanings of sounds.","At least some of the elements of language are widespread in the animal kingdom. The main thing that distinguishes human language from other species’ communication is productivity, the ability for improvising unlimited combinations of signals to represent new ideas.","A monkey might have one alarm call to indicate eagle in the air and another for snake on the ground, but it has no way to indicate eagle on the ground or snake in the tree.","Several early attempts to teach chimpanzees to talk failed.","However, chimpanzees in the wild do communicate with gestures, and investigators achieved better results by teaching them American Sign Language or other visual communication systems (Figure 13.6).","In one version, chimps learned to press keys bearing symbols to type messages on a computer, such as “Please machine give apple” or to another chimpanzee, “Please share your chocolate”. The chimpanzees’ use of symbols differed from human language in several ways.","They seldom used symbols in new, original combinations, and they used symbols mainly to request, seldom to describe.","More promising results emerged from studies of a closely related species, Pan paniscus, known as the bonobo.","Bonobos’ social order resembles humans’ in several regards.","The female is sexually responsive on almost any day and not just during her fertile period. In the mid-1980s, Sue Savage-Rumbaugh, Duane Rumbaugh, and their associates tried to teach a female bonobo named Matata to press symbols that represented words (Figure 13.7).","Although Matata made little progress, her infant son Kanzi learned just by watching her.","For example, whenever anyone said the word light, Kanzi would flip the light switch.","Kanzi and other bonobos developed language comprehension comparable to that of a typical 2- to 21⁄2-year-old child: A third reason pertains to the method of training: Learning by observation and imitation (as humans do) promotes better language understanding than the formal training methods that previous studies used.","She also learned to understand simple grammar, such as the difference between the commands “to ball take Frisbee” and “to Frisbee take ball”.","As in humans, dogs’ left hemisphere responds to words, whereas the right hemisphere responds to the emotional tone of voice.","Irene Pepperberg, a scientist well known for her work on animal cognition to improve emphasis/ She kept Alex in a stimulating environment and taught him by saying a word many times and offering rewards if Alex approximated the same sound.","In no case did she reward him with food for saying the name of something other than food.","He also answered questions of the form “How many blue keys?” in which he had to count the blue keys among objects of two shapes and two colors. At a practical level, we gain insights into how best to teach language to those who do not learn it easily.","Reconstructing the evolution of language is necessarily speculative, because no examination of fossils will help.","We can communicate just by sound (a podcast or other recording), or just visually (reading), but normal face-to-face communication combines sounds with gestures, postures, and facial expressions.","Children begin gesturing in the first year of life, and their use of gestures predicts how soon they will develop spoken language. Monkeys are known to watch one another’s mouth movements, especially when another is vocalizing, and it is plausible that the combination of sound plus mouth gesture could have been a precursor to spoken language.","Most theories fall into two categories: (1) We evolved it as a by-product of overall brain development or (2) we evolved it as a specialization.","One view is that language developed as an accidental byproduct of increased intelligence that we evolved for other reasons.","In one family, 16 people over three generations have a dominant gene that impairs their language, despite otherwise normal intelligence.","They have trouble with even simple grammatical rules, as illustrated in the following dialogue about making plurals: Psychologists would have answered “no,” until they discovered Williams syndrome, a condition affecting about 1 person in 20,000, traceable to the loss of a gene that influences connections and myelin formation in the brain.","Many people with Williams syndrome develop surprisingly better language than one might expect based on their other intellectual performance.","Their grammar may be awkward, like that of someone who learned a second language late in life. Still, the possibility of developing better language abilities than other intellect implies that language is more than a by-product of overall intelligence.","If language is not just a by-product of overall intelligence, it must have evolved as a specialized brain mechanism.","Noam Chomsky and Steven Pinker proposed that humans have a language acquisition device, a built-in mechanism for acquiring language.","Most children develop language so quickly and easily that it seems they must have been biologically prepared for this learning. Deaf children quickly learn sign language, and if no one teaches them a sign language, they invent one and teach it to one another.","The human FOXP2 gene differs from the chimpanzee version of that gene in just two bases, but those two mutations modified the human brain, jaw, and throat in ways that facilitate language development.","One speculation is that language relates to the long period of dependency in childhood. In that case, overall intelligence may be a by-product of language development more than language is a by-product of intelligence.","If humans are specially adapted to learn language, perhaps we are adapted to learn best during a sensitive period early in life, just as sparrows learn their song best during their first few months.","The consistent result is that adults have no trouble memorizing the vocabulary of a second language, but children have a great advantage on mastering grammar and pronunciation.","A massive study of more than six hundred thousand people in many countries examined second language skills as a function of current age, age of starting a second language, and total years of practice.","Another way to test the sensitive-period idea is to study people who learned no language during early childhood. Those who do not begin learning sign language until after early adolescence do not even activate the usual language areas of the brain when they communicate.","These observations clearly support the idea that childhood is a sensitive period for language development.","Studying people who have language difficulties after brain injury reveals how speech and comprehension depend on specialized but interconnected brain systems. Different injury patterns can disrupt producing fluent speech, understanding words, or other aspects of language.","Kalat introduces this approach through the history of Broca’s work and then distinguishes patterns of aphasia. A behavioral description is more informative than assuming every patient with one lesion behaves identically.","Damage to a particular brain area may produce a characteristic language impairment. Comparing injuries and symptoms helps researchers infer which regions contribute to different components of language.","Damage affecting language-production systems often produces effortful or nonfluent speech. The relationship between exact lesion location and symptoms is not one-to-one.","Other injury patterns may preserve fluency while impairing comprehension or the meaningful organization of speech.","Language depends on cooperating networks, so a single named area does not fully explain a person’s language ability or the variety of problems after brain damage.","When the man died 5 days later, Broca did an autopsy and found a lesion in the left frontal cortex.","Broca received the credit, however, because his description was more detailed and more convincing.","This discovery, the first demonstration of the function of a particular brain area, paved the way for modern neurology. When brain damage impairs language production, we call it Broca’s aphasia, or nonfluent aphasia, regardless of the exact location of damage.","Broca’s area is not sharply defined anatomically, and damage produces only minor or brief language impairment unless it extends","Almost a century and a half later, researchers using magnetic resonance imaging demonstrated that the original cases of Broca’s aphasia had damage extending deeper into the brain, including much of the basal ganglia.","Impaired Language Production People with Broca’s aphasia are impaired on all uses of language, including gestures and sign language. Prepositions, conjunctions, helping verbs, and so forth are known as the closed class of grammatical forms because a language rarely adds new prepositions, conjunctions, and the like.","In contrast, new nouns and verbs (the open class ) enter a language frequently. Problems in Comprehending Grammatical Words and Devices People with Broca’s aphasia understand most speech, except when the meaning depends on prepositions, word endings, or complex grammar—the same items that they omit when speaking.","They generally recognize that something is wrong with the sentence “He written has songs,” even if they cannot say how to improve it.","Damage in and around Wernicke’s area (Figure 13.10), located near the auditory cortex, produces Wernicke’s aphasia, characterized by poor language comprehension and impaired ability to remember the names of objects.","It is also known as fluent aphasia, because the person can still speak smoothly.","As with Broca’s aphasia, the symptoms and brain damage vary, and the damage generally extends into the thalamus and basal ganglia. We use the term Wernicke’s aphasia, or fluent aphasia, to describe a certain pattern of behavior, regardless of the location of damage.","The typical characteristics of Wernicke’s aphasia are as follows:","Although Wernicke’s area and surrounding areas are important, language comprehension also depends on the connections to other brain areas.","For example, reading the word lick activates not only Wernicke’s area but also the part of the motor cortex responsible for tongue movements. Reading throw activates the part of the premotor cortex controlling hand movements.","Many people guess that a bilingual person might rely on the left hemisphere for one language and the right hemisphere for the other.","Language production is predominantly controlled by the left-hemisphere in most people, regardless of how many languages they speak.","At first, comprehension of a second language also depends on the left hemisphere, just as much as it does for a monolingual person. However, for people who become fluent in two or more languages, especially for people who are bilingual since infancy, hemispheric control of","As with almost anything else that people do, individual differences are prominent in the ability to learn languages.","Dyslexia is an impairment of reading by someone with adequate vision, motivation, cognitive skills, and educational opportunity.","Dyslexia is especially common in English, which has so many words with irregular spellings. (Consider ought, phlegm, bivouac, khaki, yacht, choir, physique, and gnat. ) However, dyslexia occurs in all languages, and it always pertains to a difficulty converting symbols into sounds.","Many studies have reported abnormalities in the left hemisphere for people with dyslexia, even early in life, before children would be taught to read. Whereas facial recognition depends mainly on the right hemisphere, the visual word form area is more active in the left hemisphere.","For children with dyslexia, researchers have reported that the visual word form area responds less strongly than average to words, and more strongly than average to other objects.","The limitation on conclusions about the brain mechanisms of dyslexia is that most studies have been small-scale, comparing ten to twenty people with dyslexia with a similar number of typical readers. Many people with dyslexia have trouble detecting the temporal order of sounds, such as noticing the difference between beep-click-buzz and beep-buzz-click.","Many of the earliest home computers had no speech capacity, but users could plug in a device that would add speech, converting text output into sound.","Our remote ancestors didn’t just take a chimpanzee-type brain and add an independent module.","Trying to understand language is an important part of trying to understand what it means to be human.","They’re also presented in alphabetical order with definitions in the book’s Subject Index/Glossary at the end of this book. Presumably evolution equipped the left and right hemispheres with different functions for a reason.","Most people with Broca’s aphasia suffer from partial paralysis on the right side of the Pay attention to the difference between experiencing a stimulus and processing it without awareness."},{"type":"heading","id":"bp14-m13-2-study-1","text":"The central problem of consciousness"},{"type":"paragraph","text":"Consciousness refers here to awareness and experience rather than simply being awake. A person may process some information without reporting awareness of it. The relationship between physical brain events and subjective experience is part of the mind–brain problem. Monistic approaches seek to account for mental states within a physical understanding of the organism, but philosophical labels alone do not identify the neural mechanisms of awareness. Research therefore studies measurable contrasts between information that is consciously noticed and information that is not."},{"type":"heading","id":"bp14-m13-2-study-2","text":"Masking, rivalry, and unattended stimuli"},{"type":"paragraph","text":"Visual masking and binocular rivalry are examples of methods used to separate a stimulus reaching the eyes from whether it enters awareness. A stimulus can influence performance or brain responses even when a participant does not consciously report seeing it. Attention and consciousness are closely related but not identical; attention can change what becomes reportable, and unattended information may still be processed. Good experiments distinguish the physical input from the subjective report and the behavioral task."},{"type":"heading","id":"bp14-m13-2-study-3","text":"Timing and brain activity"},{"type":"paragraph","text":"Researchers compare the timing of neural events, decisions, and subjective awareness to infer how conscious processing unfolds. Brain measures may predict a response before someone can describe deciding, but interpreting this requires care about task design and how awareness was measured. Different cortical and subcortical systems participate in attention and conscious report. No single measurement has yet provided a complete explanation of subjective experience."},{"type":"heading","id":"bp14-m13-2-study-4","text":"Spatial neglect and clinical evidence"},{"type":"paragraph","text":"Brain injury can produce spatial neglect, in which a person fails to attend to or respond to parts of space despite the absence of ordinary blindness in the neglected area. Such cases reveal how awareness and attention can depend on specific networks. They also demonstrate that failure to report a stimulus is not equivalent to proving that every stage of processing was absent.
